# Integrated Remediation Plan — Profiler and Classroom, Phase 0 through Phase 6

**Agreed 2026-09-07 in the X3 close-out session (Opus 5 xhigh, v05.01r) and persisted at Phase 0 (Fable 5.1 High, v05.02r).** Until this file existed the plan lived only in a chat transcript and in the *Latest Session* of `SESSION-CONTEXT.md`; Phase 3's curriculum-skeleton review had nothing to edit against. This is now the **live run order for the remaining program**. **§7 (agreed 2026-09-07 PM, persisted at v05.08r) extends that order from Phase 3's close to completion and supersedes §2's rows 4–6 and G6 where they differ**; §2–§4 are kept as the v05.01r record. `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §9.5 rows 5–6 and §6 (Phase D), and `CLASSROOM-CURRICULUM-PLAN.md` §7 (the cut line), map onto Phases 0–4 below and are kept as their own records, not as competing schedules.

**The model rule is unchanged** — `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §2: Medium buys comparison of records the repo already holds; High buys reading depth; Opus 5 xhigh buys research and authoring; Fable 5.1 xhigh buys design and evaluation against a corpus. Every phase below names its model and effort for that reason, and the reason is written down.

**Two rules that shaped the order, both learned the hard way at X3:**

1. **Cheap-and-certain before slow-and-uncertain.** The first recommendation out of X3 named only a `sungrow` refresh, when five findings needed no research at all. The single-recommendation rule picked the highest-value item instead of the cheapest. Phase 1 (zero-research fixes) therefore precedes Phase 2a (research).
2. **The session that writes a criterion does not close against it.** X3 was marked Done against an exit criterion the same session wrote in the same commit. Phase 0 exists to reverse that, and every "done when" below is closed by the developer or by a later session, never by the one that authored it.

## 1 · The finding set this plan remediates

All of these were produced by X3 (v05.01r) reading the 21 over-cap cross-reference scopes and the 38 aged report pins. **None was fixed there, deliberately** — step 7 of the Profiler Command requires two figures that may describe the same thing to be *stated*, not silently reconciled, and X3 was a read-and-report item. Each finding is assigned to exactly one phase.

## 3 · Per-phase detail and the reason for each model

**Phase 0 — Fable 5.1 High.** The work is reading a checker and a schema carefully enough to state *why* a clause is unreachable, then changing the checker without breaking its other jobs. That is reading depth (High), not research (Opus) and not design (xhigh). Done at v05.02r; the evidence is in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §9.3 "Phase 0 evaluation" and §9.4.

**Phase 1 — Fable 5.1 Medium.** Every correcting fact is already in another dossier: Intersect's 10-Q allocation, Brookfield/Bloom's USD 25bn, Arevon's own project list, Lightsource's supplier edge. §2 of the coverage plan defines Medium as comparison of records the repo already holds, and this is that. It goes before 2a because it is cheap and certain; the X3 session's first recommendation got this order wrong. **Side-effect to expect and leave alone:** revising `hithium` moves it past v11, so its three verified pins (`grid-scale-bess`, `named-project-bess-attach`, `s154-listed-bess-suppliers`) re-warn. That is the Phase 0 mechanism working; the pins are read again at Phase 6 (or by whichever session next reads them), not silenced.

**Phase 2a — Opus 5 xhigh.** Two dossiers need facts the corpus does not hold: Sungrow's own SST / grid-to-chip product record and its FCC-rule position, and CATL's Zhonhen holdco purchase from CATL's side. New sourcing is research, and the model rule puts research on Opus. It follows Phase 1 so the cheap fixes are not held hostage to the slow ones.

**Phase 2b — Fable 5.1 High.** The tract/Prime disagreement is the one finding where the wrong fix is worse than no fix: a USD 4bn campus total and a USD 400m phase-1 figure can both be true. The task is to read the cited sources and state the scopes, which is reading depth. It is split from 2a because it needs no new sources and a different model.

**Phase 3 — Fable 5.1 xhigh.** The curriculum was cut on an 89-dossier corpus and the corpus is now 154 with three of the plan's own exclusions contradicted. Re-planning a curriculum against a corpus is design and evaluation, which is what xhigh on Fable buys. Phase 3 edits against §4 below; without this file it had no skeleton to edit.

**Phase 4 — Opus 5 xhigh, one lesson per session.** The standing decision from the Classroom build: lesson authoring is corpus synthesis with provenance stamps and a contract to satisfy, and it has never been done more than one per session without a pipeline. Twenty sessions.

**Phase 5 — Fable 5.1 xhigh.** Whether to regenerate, re-scope or retire a report is a strategy question, and the reports' defect is scope rather than fact (F11). Evaluating four reports against a corpus that nearly doubled is design work. It runs before Phase 6 so the four reports are authored once, not twice — the X3 session's explicit reason for *not* regenerating at v05.01r.

**Phase 6 — Opus 5 xhigh.** Report-Command authoring: synthesis from dossiers with verbatim citations and a coverage block, on the writing style in `PROFILER-STYLES.md`. Opus per the model rule. The superseded editions stay on disk and, since Phase 0, are no longer drift-checked, so the checker's count after Phase 6 reflects only the new editions.

**G6 — blocked on the developer.** Eight guidance analyses exist and none is a large-load-interconnection subject; the `industry guidance:` command needs a document to ingest. No model choice is pending.

## 5 · Dependencies, standing caveats, and what is recorded but not scheduled

**Dependencies.**
- Phase 0 first; nothing depends on it, but every later phase reads the record it corrects.
- Phase 1 before 2a (cheap-and-certain first). 2a and 2b are independent of each other and of Phase 3.
- Phase 3 before Phase 4; Phase 4's twenty sessions can interleave with 2a/2b/5/6 — they touch different files.
- Phase 5 before Phase 6, always. Regenerating before the evaluation authors the reports twice.
- G6 runs whenever the developer supplies the document; it never blocks a phase.

**Standing caveats while the plan runs.**
- **~~Do not act on the `named-project` report's whitespace call as a *storage* whitespace call.~~ LIFTED at Phase 6 session 1 (v05.13r, 2026-09-08)** — `named-project-bess-attach--opportunity--2026-09-08` supersedes the 2026-08-30 edition and states the call in its own words: the whitespace is **supplier** whitespace, storage is in the plan at four of the nine projects with no supplier named at any of them, and the party to court is the storage-scope holder rather than the hyperscaler. The record the caveat carried, kept for provenance: Storage is in the plan at Lighthouse (`vantage` v9: 70% of power from new solar, wind and battery storage built for the project) and at Project Jupiter (`stack-infrastructure` v7: a microgrid with battery energy storage), and Meta's BESS exposure runs through a utility-tolled lane (`meta` v9: Enbridge Cowboy, 200 MW / 1,600 MWh); no BESS supplier is named at any of the nine registered projects except Trimount (Hithium) and Colossus (Tesla). The whitespace that survives is **supplier** whitespace, and the party to court at Lighthouse and Jupiter is whoever holds the storage scope in those plans, not the hyperscaler. That edition has now landed, so this caveat no longer constrains any phase; the 2026-09-08 edition is the statement of record.
- **Reports are immutable.** No phase edits a published report; the only route to a fresh pin is a new edition carrying `supersedes`. A pin that re-warns after a dossier bump is read again and recorded in `report-pins-verified.json`, never re-pinned.
- **X3b closed at v05.03r** — the developer accepted the amended criterion in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §9.3 on 2026-09-07, and Phase X is closed. The pins that re-warn after Phase 1 are read again and recorded, never re-pinned.
- **CHANGELOG rotation** is decided by the non-exempt count on the day a push lands (`CHANGELOG-archive.md` step 1, fixed at v05.01r). When it fires, `git fetch --unshallow` runs before any SHA lookup.

**Recorded, not scheduled.**
- `microsoft` v4 omits the 1.35 GW Monarch LOI that `anthropic` and `nscale` attribute to it. Defensible: never confirmed, both counterparties mark it press-sourced. Revisit only if Microsoft confirms.
- The `check-profiler-crossrefs.py` detector recommendation from X3 (§9.3 of the coverage plan): an `A-on-B versus B-on-B` figure comparison behind a flag with calibration first; `--no-cap` as an audit switch; `OQ_SCOPE_MAX` stays at 900. Needs its own calibration session; not on this plan's critical path.
- Standing, unassigned housekeeping carried in `SESSION-CONTEXT.md`: the README archive listing lags; `archive/nvidia.profile.v2.json` is missing and unreconstructable; OSHA IMIS and SEC EDGAR are network-blocked from the remote environment.

### 7.2 · Decisions taken 2026-09-07 PM

**The segment layer (approved as proposed in chat).** Classroom gains a generated market-structure layer between the study guides and the reports: one public **segment lesson** per value-chain segment (players' stated positions, numbers, relationship map, what moved, the policy fence), generated by a script from the registry, dossiers and graph and regenerated when a member dossier moves; and one contributor-tier **landscape module** per segment (who dominates and on what basis, who threatens, each player's strategy bet, indicators, the seller's play), authored by Opus in the guidance-module shape. Three fences moved deliberately, all approved:

1. The content contract is amended: mechanism lessons keep "never company trivia"; segment lessons may drill *structure*; a separate **roster deck** of player cards is opt-in.
2. Landscape analyses sit at the **contributor** tier (guidance-module shape), not admin (report shape), so the future team reads them.
3. The **segments** become a registry layer — `profiler-segments.json`, segment → members with a role (incumbent / challenger / adjacent) — that the Profiler Command must populate on every new dossier, mirrored into the registry as `segments[]`.

**The taxonomy** is the seventeen segments proposed in chat (cells and chemistry; storage integrators and containers; power conversion and rack-power silicon; grid equipment; in-hall power; bridge and on-site generation; clean firm and nuclear; cooling; compute and the rack; EPC and construction with GCs; storage developers and IPPs; AIDC developers and landlords; hyperscalers and AI labs; neoclouds; utilities; capital; advisors) **plus two new ones and one merge**, decided with the five empty areas:

| Area | Decision | Companies approved (hypotheses — every session verifies against sources before writing, per `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §5's rule) | Model under §2 |
|------|----------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------|
| Software and optimization | **New segment** | Stem · Habitat Energy · Gridmatic | Stem public (NYSE: STEM) → Opus 5 xhigh; Habitat and Gridmatic private → Fable 5.1 High |
| Insurance and risk transfer | **New segment** (closes the G10 residual) | Marsh (Marsh McLennan) · Aon · kWh Analytics | Marsh, Aon public → Opus 5 xhigh; kWh Analytics private → Fable 5.1 High |
| Testing and certification | **Merged with advisors into "assurance"**, which lifts advisors above the floor | UL Solutions · CSA Group · Intertek | UL Solutions (NYSE: ULS) and Intertek (LSE: ITRK) → Opus 5 xhigh; CSA Group (private non-profit) → Fable 5.1 High |
| Upstream materials | **Adjacent role on cells and chemistry**, not a segment yet | Albemarle (lithium) · a US LFP-cathode entrant — **Mitra Chem** recommended, ICL (St. Louis LFP plant) as the public alternate, Nano One as the North-American fallback · a graphite/anode name — **Novonix** (Chattanooga synthetic graphite; ASX/Nasdaq) recommended, Syrah Resources' Vidalia plant as the operating-US alternate, Westwater Resources' Kellyton as the domestic natural-graphite alternate | Albemarle, Novonix, ICL, Syrah, Westwater public → Opus 5 xhigh; Mitra Chem private → Fable 5.1 High |
| Transmission developers | **Adjacent role on grid equipment and utilities** | Grid United · Pattern Energy | Both private (Pattern under CPP Investments) → Fable 5.1 High |
| Cell roster (C12's absentees, existing segment) | Coverage item | CALB · Cornex · Great Power | CALB (HKEX: 3931) and Great Power (SZSE: 300438) → Opus 5 xhigh; Cornex private → Fable 5.1 High |

That is **nineteen segments** and **seventeen dossiers with study guides** to fill *(nineteen after the developer's 2026-09-07 PM taxonomy review added AMD and Supermicro as group E7 for `compute-and-the-rack`)* (three per session where the research frame is shared → six to seven sessions). The **floor rule**: no landscape module is authored for a segment until it holds at least three dossiers including one incumbent and one challenger. Today only assurance (two advisors) is below it among the nineteen; the two new segments start at zero.

**G6 — research-synthesis accepted.** One Opus 5 xhigh guidance session on the federal interconnection layer (FERC Order 2023, large-load tariffs as a class, study-fee regimes), composed against the six utility guides rather than restating them; the module unlocks the contributor lesson behind `the-fence-line` (Phase 4 row 26). The register's "blocked on a developer-supplied document" wording is corrected by S0.

One `Updated to` accounts for one; **one consumption is unaccounted for**, and the only Scraper deploy activity in between was v05.83r's. **So §7.26's middle case is wrong as stated**: `Already up to date` is the state the deployment reports at **read** time, not a statement that the call spent nothing — v05.83r's *unconfirmed first GET leg had already run `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()`* and consumed a deployment, and the poll then read back a bundle that was by then current. **The correction, stated as the inference it is**: `Updated to` consumes one; a step that never fires consumes none and logs nothing; **`Already up to date` is silent on consumption and must not be forecast as zero.** The v05.84r conclusion was inferred from wording and never measured across the event; this is the first measurement that spans it. (ii) **A track's `short` is not automatically extended by an insertion, and the test is what kind of sentence it is.** `market-access`'s is §3.5's verbatim finished-track outcome; `aidc-campus`'s is a running "So far: …" enumeration. Row 9 extended the latter and row 6 correctly left the former alone, so this row left it alone too and recorded the reasoning rather than repeating the judgement call. Its 4 × 8 table rendered clean at 1400px — a twelfth data point against the open `.cl-tbl` question and the first with a `{{term}}` span in the first column. **The deploy added a third case to the counter rule** (recorded at v05.86r): Classroom confirmed on the first GET leg at `Updated to v01.41g (deployment 52) | 52/200` with no POLL and no probe — the ninth consecutive clean use of the §7.19 order — while the **Scraper step never fired at all**, because each project's step opens with `git diff --name-only "$PRE" HEAD | grep -q "$GS" || exit 0` and `Scraper.gs` was not in the merge diff. So `Updated to` consumes one, `Already up to date` consumes none, and **a step that never fires consumes none and logs nothing** — forecast the counter from whether the bundle is behind, but forecast the log line from whether the file is in the diff. Phase 4 is **10 of 26**. Brief was §7.26; §7.27 written in the same commit and its counters refreshed in both halves at v05.86r. **Next by §7.4 item 6's interleave, now at 10:6, is Phase 4 row 11 (`contracts-and-revenue`) — its brief is §7.28** |

### 7.4 · Sequencing and the critical path

1. **S0** first — every other new item reads its files.
2. **K1** early — a live defect independent of everything else; it can run before S0.
3. **Phase 5, then S1, then Phase 6** — the evaluation is one session and the reports feed the landscape modules' admin extras, so they are fresh before S2 starts.
4. **S3's critical path — sessions 1–5 of `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §10.4** (E7 → E2a → E2b+E3b → E1a → E1b) before the S2 sessions for compute, insurance, assurance and software; the remaining five deepen segments already at the floor and run whenever a Profiler session is free. Note the ordering the floor arithmetic forces: **assurance and insurance are both blocked on a private-company challenger**, so the Fable session `E2b+E3b` gates two landscapes that no Opus session can unblock.
5. **G6**, then **C3** before S2 passes about six modules. *(Re-sequenced 2026-09-13, v05.42r, at the close of S3: G6 and C3 now run **before any** S2 module — the table above — so that the homecoming migrates nine modules and no landscapes.)*
6. **S2** interleaved with **Phase 4** rows — the two long lanes — with Phase 4 row 1 (`four-machines`) early because it creates `electrical-foundations`.
7. **K2** once segment lessons exist; **C5** after about six landscapes; **C6** when the team exists.

**Standing caveats carried forward:** the `named-project` report's whitespace call was restated by Phase 5 as *supplier* whitespace (§5, §7.7) and lifts with Phase 6 session 1's edition; reports stay immutable; the CHANGELOG rotation is decided by count on the day a push lands; the register is re-run and dated, never remembered (S3 closes G10's residual only when a session re-runs the row).

### 7.7 · Phase 5 decisions (2026-09-08, v05.12r — Fable 5.1 xhigh)

Evaluated against the corpus as it stands: **154 dossiers, 9 registered named projects, the 19-segment registry (283 memberships)**, and `check-profiler-reports.py` at **0 errors / 7 warnings** before and after (identical output: `hithium` v13 ×3, `sungrow` v9, `catl` v7 ×2, `zhonhen` v7). No report file, `reports-index.json`, `report-pins-verified.json`, dossier, guide, lesson, registry or Routine was touched. Phase 5 closes here against the "done when" §2 wrote at X3 (v05.01r) — a criterion this session did not author, so the §6 flip below is the later-session close rule 2 requires.

**What the read established, across all four reports**

1. **The defect is scope, not fact (F11 confirmed).** Of the 45 coverage pins, 38 were read or compared at X3 and 35 held exactly; the three that did not (F10) recorded the corpus moving *under* a report, not a wrong figure. The seven warnings since are Phase 1 / 2a moves, and each was read again here against what the reports cite: `hithium` v12 added the `lightsource-bp` customer edge and v13 marked its "most architecturally complete grid-to-chip offer" as Hithium's own assessment — no report cites either, and every cited Hithium figure (FY2024 RMB 12.917B / USD 1,800m, Trimount 2.8 GWh, the Texas plant, the 27 Apr 2026 HKEX lapse, the ~10 CATL suits) is untouched; `sungrow` v9 folded the H1 2026 interim and the first-party **EnerNeo** SST — the grid-scale report's cited figures hold (Wood Mackenzie #1, USD 12,408m, PowerTitan 3.0, Masdar 7.5 GWh) while the AIDC report's "SST roster still forming" framing is now contradicted by a covered company it left out of scope; `catl` v7 added the RMB 4.1B / 49% Zhonhen holdco **capital increase** with the CATL-side null — the grid-scale KPI (USD 58,951m) and every §154 citation hold; `zhonhen` v7 replaced an unsourced "~43.5% premium" that no report cited, and the RMB 4.1B / 49% the AIDC report's Judgment 4 rests on survives as a capital increase rather than a purchase. **Every warning is a pin to re-read, not a judgment to withdraw; not one is re-pinned.**
2. **Version drift and substantive drift are different quantities, and the reports show it.** Scoped dossiers moved past their pins: named-project **13 of 15** (all but `oracle` v4 and `xai` v4), AIDC **8 of 8**, grid-scale **12 of 12**, §154 **5 of 5**. Scoped dossiers carrying a `recentDevelopments[]` entry dated after the edition's `generated`: **0 of 15, 0 of 8, 1 of 12 (`sungrow`, 4 Sep), 0 of 5.** The moves are the schema v7 backfill (v03.93r), the project-pin pass (v03.94r), X2's relationship curation (v04.72r) and the Phase 1 / 2a fixes — depth added to the record, not events. F10 itself lived in `technicalSpecs` and `productsAndServices` (Lighthouse's "solar, wind, battery storage" in `vantage` v9; Project Jupiter's "microgrid … with battery energy storage" in `stack-infrastructure` v7; Enbridge Cowboy's 200 MW / 1,600 MWh Tesla BESS in `meta` v9), which no count of versions or developments would have surfaced — X3's wide-gap *read* found it. This is the evidence behind the Routine decision below.
3. **The project web moved more than the pins did.** At generation the named-project report's scope was the 15 companies whose curated relationships pinned eight projects. Today **24 dossiers pin nine projects**: ten are outside the report's scope — `aep`, `caterpillar`, `crusoe`, `ge-vernova`, `samsung-ct` (Stargate / Abilene), `anthropic` and `caterpillar` (Colossus), `mccarthy` and `whiting-turner` (Lighthouse's four-way GC bench), `google` and `hut-8` (River Bend, registered at v04.83r and absent from the report) — and one scoped company, `tesla`, pins nothing (its Megapack sale is cited through `xai`, as X3 found). **No BESS supplier is named at any Stargate campus, at Hyperion or at Homer City in any of the 24 dossiers** — the report's "None named" cells survive literally; what has changed is that storage is now *in the plan* at two of them.
4. **The 800 VDC roster is roughly fifteen covered companies, not eight (F11's count confirmed).** NVIDIA's own dossier carries partner edges to `abb`, `ge-vernova` and `hitachi-energy` (grid tier), `vertiv` and `eaton` (founding), `delta-electronics`, `liteon`, `megmeet`, `infineon` and `flex` (component tier); dossier mentions of 800 VDC run `vicor` 79, `zhonhen` 56, `delta-electronics` 54, `flex` 44, `infineon` 41, `megmeet` 33, `liteon` 32, `abb` 23, `vertiv` 22, `eaton` 21, `hitachi-energy` 19, `sungrow` 19 (EnerNeo), `ge-vernova` 17, `schneider-electric` 13. Seven of those fifteen post-date the report or were excluded from it. The segment registry's `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` (19 members) is the wider set; the 800 VDC roster is the peer family the report's question actually names.
5. **The integrator peer family is the registry's, and it is eighteen, not twelve.** `storage-integrators-and-containers` holds ten incumbents and eight challengers, all of which existed when the report was cut at twelve: `envision-energy` (Wood Mackenzie tied-#5), `crrc-zhuzhou`, `hyperstrong`, `trina-storage`, `jinko`, `sunwoda`, `ls-energy-solutions` and `prevalon` were left out; `flexgen` is now `adjacent` (software) and `eve-energy` is placed in cells and in-hall power, not in the integrator set. **S2's first landscape is this segment**, so this is the report whose scope matters most to S2.
6. **The §154 statutory six are now all covered.** `gotion` (v04.94r) closes the report's own stated gap; `rept` v1 records that it is *not* named — a useful negative the edition can state. No other dossier is §154(b)-listed.
7. **Two things the brief did not anticipate, recorded rather than fixed.** *(a)* **`s154-listed-bess-suppliers--risk--2026-08-29.report.json` was edited after publication** — commit `e1324c7` (v04.94r, session C12) rewrote its `scope.rationale` to say Gotion "is covered … this report predates it and its scope was not re-cut; X3 owns the re-pin". The schema forbids editing a published report; the checker cannot see a prose edit. This session did not revert it (that would be a second edit of an immutable file); Phase 6's supersession moots it, and whether to restore the published text is the developer's call. *(b)* **The earnings desk is leaving rows overdue.** The calendar shows `sinexcel` (due 2026-08-11), `eve-energy` (08-20), `iren` (08-27), `jinko` (08-27) and `byd` (08-29) past due; the desk's 7 September run (`SUCCEEDED`, 19 minutes) landed no commit. Three of those rows are indicators the reports name (BYD H1 2026 for grid-scale and §154; EVE's H1 and H-share outcome for §154; Sinexcel's H1 for AIDC), and `megmeet` / `zhonhen` H1 2026 interims (statutory deadline 31 Aug) are not in their dossiers either, with calendar rows pointing at 30 Oct. This is the same failure shape as the 7-for-7 the desk replaced; it is not Phase 5's to fix, but Phase 6's preflight (Report Command step 2: refresh first or proceed with staleness flagged) inherits it.
8. **A sequencing gap in §10.6, resolved here.** The curriculum plan says a landscape's admin extras "reach admins only through a report's `guidanceOverlays[]` anchored on the module's sections", and §7.4 puts Phase 6 before S2 so that landscapes do not anchor on a retired edition. Mechanically the anchor points the other way: an overlay lives in the *report* and names a `moduleId` / `sectionId` that `check-profiler-reports.py` validates against the live modules, so a Phase 6 edition **cannot** overlay a landscape S2 has not yet written, and an immutable edition cannot gain the overlay later. Decision: Phase 6 editions overlay the **existing** guidance modules (as the three overlay-bearing reports do today); an S2 landscape records the current edition's id in its `claims-ledger` / `read-next` as a `report:` reference; the overlays onto `landscape-<id>-YYYY-MM` sections arrive with the **next** edition of each report after that landscape exists (the monthly Routine for the opportunity series; the quarterly guidance review for the other three). Phase 6 before S2 still holds — for the *edition* the landscapes cite, not for the lens.
9. **No Phase E row changes any answer.** E6 (CALB · Cornex · Great Power) fills `cells-and-chemistry`, and no report is scoped on cells — the grid-scale scope is the integrator set, and none of the three is §154(b)-listed. E7 (AMD · Supermicro) fills `compute-and-the-rack`, downstream of the 800 VDC conversion layer the AIDC report ranks. E1–E5 touch software, insurance, assurance, materials and transmission. **None of the four editions waits for Phase E**; the registry on the day, not the ledger, decides membership.

**None of the four is retired.** Each answers a question the corpus still asks and S2 will cite; each fails only on scope. **Regenerate-as-is was rejected for all four** because regenerating the old scope would re-pin a known under-count.

**The drift-gated monthly Routine (`trig_01TvnXREVHsQ4QCrtveYjvZM`) — REPLACE the measure, keep the cadence.** *Recorded only; the developer applies it — nothing was created, updated or deleted.*
- **Keep:** monthly on the 1st, fresh session, the opportunity series only (the other three reports stay on the quarterly guidance review with the library's age chip — fresh ≤45d / aging ≤120d / stale — as `profiler-app.md` already says), stand down silently with a one-line count, and "What changed since the last edition" as the new edition's first section.
- **Replace the gate.** Today's gate — *fewer than 10 scoped dossiers revised past their pins and no fired indicator → stand down* — is a churn detector at 154 companies: **it is already crossed (13 of 15) while 0 of 15 scoped dossiers carry a development since the edition and 35 of 38 pins held exactly**, and it would have missed F10, which no version count reaches. The desk at three refreshes a day and a corpus that doubles by phases (v03.93r moved 83 dossiers in one commit) guarantee the count trips on housekeeping. New gate — author when **any** of: **(a)** an `indicators[]` event is confirmed (unchanged); **(b) scope drift** — `profiler-projects.json` holds a project the edition does not cover, **or** the set of dossiers pinning registered projects (`relationships[].project`) differs from the edition's `scope.value` by **three or more** companies; **(c) substantive pin drift** — **five or more** scoped dossiers carry a `recentDevelopments[]` entry absent from the archived pinned version (`archive/<slug>.profile.v<pinned>.json`; dated after `generated` where no archive exists). The raw count of dossiers past their pins is still reported in the stand-down line — for information, never as a gate. Thresholds: three companies is ~12% of a 25-company scope and would have fired on River Bend alone (two new pinning dossiers plus a new project); five developments is ~20%, chosen so the 2026-10-01 fire on Phase 6's fresh edition stands down unless something happened.
- **Timing.** Until the gate is changed, the **2026-10-01 fire authors an edition on churn** unless Phase 6's edition has superseded the 30 Aug one first (a fresh edition resets the count to 0). Either land Phase 6 session 1 before then or pause the Routine until it has; do not let the Routine author what Opus is about to author (§3's "authored once, not twice").
- **Not extended.** A second Routine over the three competitive/risk reports is not recommended: the quarterly review already reads them, and the same three-signal test is its supersession cue.

**The §5 caveat — RESTATED, not lifted.** The plan's words are now in §5: the whitespace is **supplier** whitespace, not storage whitespace, at Lighthouse and Project Jupiter; storage-in-the-plan is on the record at both (`vantage` v9, `stack-infrastructure` v7) and at Meta through a utility-tolled lane (`meta` v9); no BESS supplier is named at any of the nine projects except Trimount (Hithium) and Colossus (Tesla). The caveat lifts when Phase 6 session 1's edition supersedes `named-project-bess-attach--opportunity--2026-08-30`.

**Phase 6 inherits this order:** session 1 — the named-project edition; session 2 — the grid-scale and §154 editions together, after the `byd` / `eve-energy` H1 2026 refreshes; session 3 — the AIDC edition, after the `megmeet` / `zhonhen` / `sinexcel` H1 2026 refreshes. Three sessions, within §7.3's 2–4. The brief for each is §7.8.

### 7.11 · Paste-in brief for C3 session 3 (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-13 at v05.47r, at the close of C3 session 2

The last C3 session. Sessions 1 and 2 landed the content, the read ops, the guidance surface, the Admin lens and the token-gated mentions route (v05.44r–v05.47r); the slice plan under the C3 bullet of `PHASE6-CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md` leaves **items 4, 6 and 7** here, plus the two the checklist implies but does not number — Profiler's guidance deep-links redirect to Classroom, and Profiler's own copy of the modules is deleted. This is the session that ends the duplicate window, so it is the one where a mistake is not recoverable by falling back to the other app.

**Facts established by sessions 1–2 that this brief depends on** (re-verify, do not assume): the shared `GUIDANCE_PEER_TOKEN` **is set and working in both projects** — the dossier chips are live and were confirmed by the developer on the Tesla and NVIDIA dossiers at v05.47r. `guidanceMentions_()` already lives **only** in `Classroom.gs`, so Profiler's copy is **twelve** functions, not thirteen, and `scripts/check-guidance-parity.py` watches exactly those twelve. Profiler's progress store is one Script Property per account, `gd_progress:<email>`, and it holds **three kinds of doc id**: module ids, `study-<slug>` and `dossier-<slug>`. Classroom's is `cl_progress:<email>` (`CL_PROGRESS_PROP_PREFIX`). Values are **completion dates** (`'YYYY-MM-DD'`), with legacy `true` accepted forever and never rewritten.

**The trap in item 4.** `gd_progress:` is not a guidance store — it is a shared store that guidance, study guides and dossiers all write into. A migration that moves the whole property moves study and dossier ticks into Classroom, where nothing reads them and where they will be silently lost the next time either side writes. **Move only the keys whose doc id is a registered module id**, leave everything else in Profiler untouched, and make the import idempotent: it must be safe to run twice, and a Classroom tick that is already newer than the Profiler one must win rather than being overwritten. "Developer study history is not resettable state" is the design's phrasing and it is the whole reason this is a one-shot admin operation rather than a lazy merge.

**The trap in item 6.** The quarterly review Routine is `Industry Guidance quarterly review` (`trig_01CrhxzfBV6uKQNKpUXLLMSZ`, cron `0 13 15 1,4,7,10 *`, fresh session per fire). Re-pointing it is **developer-approved before it happens** — do not update, delete or recreate it without an explicit yes in the session, and never delete-and-recreate (that loses its run history).

**The trap in the deletion.** Deleting the twelve functions from `Profiler.gs` breaks `handleGuidanceOp_`'s `gop=index|doc` and every page surface above them — the masthead `✦ Industry Guidance` button, `#ov-guide-overlay`, `gdRenderDoc`, the search, the unified glossary, and `ovGuideOpenDoc`, which is what the **dossier mention chips call when clicked**. Those chips must keep working: after this session they should deep-link into Classroom (`Classroom.html#guidance/<id>`) rather than open a Profiler overlay. Decide deliberately how much of Profiler's `gd*` renderer goes and say so in the record — the Admin lens's `gdApplyLens`/`gdLoadAdminLens` are part of that engine and Profiler's own report view still needs the section primitives.

### 7.12 · Paste-in brief for the bankability review (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-13 at v05.49r, at the close of C3

Order **3** of the §7.3 run table, and the first session after C3. It was scheduled beside C3 precisely because the module it edits moved during C3; now that the migration is closed, **it edits one file instead of two** and `check-guidance-parity.py` is not there to fail the build if it gets that wrong — because there is nothing left to keep in parity.

**Facts established by C3 that this brief depends on** (re-verify, do not assume): `guidanceDocBankability_()` lives **only** in `googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.gs`, below the `// CONTENT END` fence. Its module id is `bess-bankability-2026-08`, `updated` 2026-08-24, `reviewBy` **2026-10-01** — the only module inside `check-classroom-curriculum.py`'s 30-day horizon. Its eleven section ids are `what`, `stack`, `ninefortya`, `fires`, `bankability`, `counterparty`, `checklist`, `sales`, `ledger`, `cards`, `quiz`. **Three reports anchor `guidanceOverlays[]` at `counterparty`** — `grid-scale-bess--competitive--2026-09-08`, `s154-listed-bess-suppliers--risk--2026-09-08` and the superseded `…2026-08-29` — and the two current ones render as the two Admin-lens panels an admin sees in that section.

**The trap C3 created, and it is new since the last time anyone edited a module.** A module's section ids are no longer just anchors. Since v01.23g they are **progress-tick keys** in `cl_progress:<email>` and they are **embedded in C4 drill item ids** (`gc:<moduleId>:<sectionId>:<n>`, `gq:…`). Renaming or removing a section id therefore (a) orphans every account's tick for it — `clProgressVisible_` drops ticks whose section no longer exists, deliberately, so a rename reads as lost history rather than moved history; (b) invalidates that section's drill items and their SM-2 schedules; and (c) breaks the three report overlay anchors above. **Do not rename or delete a section id.** Edit prose inside a section freely; add a new section if the review needs one.

**The second-order effect, which is by design and should not be "fixed".** Editing a flashcard's or quiz item's text changes `clDrillHash_`, and `clDrillQueue_` treats a hash change as NEW — the item loses its schedule and is re-introduced. That is the intended rule ("the text moved under the schedule, so the schedule is not evidence of anything"). Expect it, say so in the record, do not work around it.

> Run the bankability review — order 3 of INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md §7.3 — on Opus 5 xhigh as a fresh session. This is the quarterly-freshness review of ONE Industry Guidance module, `bess-bankability-2026-08` (`reviewBy` 2026-10-01), now that C3 has moved guidance into Classroom. It authors no new module, no lesson, no landscape and no dossier, edits no registry, and publishes no report.

### 7.14 · Paste-in brief for Phase 4 row 4 (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-13 at v05.55r, at the close of row 3

Phase 4 is now **three of twenty-six**, and the registry work is finished: every track the curriculum plan specifies exists, so rows 4–24 author a lesson and nothing else. That makes the checker signature predictable — **P1 on the plan files and, when the lesson lands mid-track, P5 on the `lessons[]` insertion; never P3.** A run that sees anything else should stop and read why.

**What the last three runs established, so this one does not rediscover it.** The render harness answers the ops from the **real serving functions** — the PROJECT region run in Node, `clTrackIndexFor_` / `clLessonIndexFor_` / `clTrackFor_` / `clStudyNext_` replayed through a `window._gasPost` stub — which is what makes the render evidence of what the server would send rather than of a fixture. `clStudyNext_` is the proof a lesson is on the teaching walk, and for a mid-track insertion it also shows whose pointer moved. The Actions log API truncates before the Deploy step on a run this size, so the authoritative deploy check is `curl` against `?action=api&op=deploy`. And the EST clock, not UTC, governs the CHANGELOG rotation rule — the two diverge for five hours every evening.

**One hazard found at row 3 — now a checker, so this paragraph is background rather than an instruction.** `clFmt` in `Classroom.html` resolves `**bold**` with `/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/g`, which **cannot contain an asterisk**, so *italic nested inside bold* silently fails and renders literal asterisks to the reader. Use one level of emphasis per span. **`check-classroom-content.py` errors on it as of v05.56r** — across lessons, tracks and the nine guidance modules — so no manual simulation is needed; a clean checker run is the check. *(The manual recipe this paragraph carried at v05.55r named too wide a scope: it listed `tiles`, `glossary`, `short` and `title`, which reach the DOM through `textContent` and would have produced false positives. The implemented scope is narrower and kind-aware — see `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` → Verification.)*

That failure is deferred on purpose so the merge and branch cleanup still run, and **that design must not be "tidied up" into failing in place.** **Corrected 2026-09-13 at v05.58r, from run #568 — the wording this brief carried at v05.57r said a red gate "means the deploy did not land", and that is false in one direction.** Run #568 went red on Classroom and the deploy *had* landed: `.github/scripts/gas-deploy.sh` fires one GET (`--max-time 120`) and, failing that, one POST, with **no poll afterwards**, so when the GET leg outruns its budget the confirmation is lost even though Apps Script finishes the update seconds later. `Classroom.gs` is now ~1.9 MB and is the slowest project in the repo, so it is the one that hits this. **The consequence is the part that matters:** the `deploy` job requires `auto-merge.result == 'success'`, so a red gate **skips the GitHub Pages deploy entirely** — `Classroomgs.version.txt` and `Classroomgs.changelog.md` stay stale on the live site even though the GAS project is current. **So: always confirm independently** with `curl "https://script.google.com/macros/s/$(jq -r .DEPLOYMENT_ID googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.config.json)/exec?action=api&op=deploy"` — `Already up to date (<version>)` means it landed — and also check `https://lightaisolutions.github.io/Sales/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt`. If the GAS deploy landed but Pages is stale, the documented recovery is a **`workflow_dispatch` on `main`**: `auto-merge` is gated `github.ref != 'refs/heads/main'` so it skips, and `deploy` then fires through its `auto-merge.result == 'skipped' && is-initialized` branch. That is what run #569 did, and it went green.
>
> CHANGELOG — **use the EST clock, not UTC.** `TZ=America/New_York date '+%Y-%m-%d'` is what the rotation rule means by "today"; the harness clock may already have rolled over while EST has not, and firing rotation early is a real error. Sections dated today (EST) are exempt; rotation fires when the NON-EXEMPT count reaches 100. **A remote session's clone is shallow** — check `git rev-list --count HEAD` and run `git fetch --unshallow origin main` before any SHA lookup, because unshallowing does not persist between containers. Oldest whole date group: thirteen sections dated 2026-09-04. `Profilerhtml.changelog.md` is at 49/50 but this session must not touch Profiler. Normal Pre-Commit and Pre-Push checklists; `claude/*` branch, `git ls-remote` before each push, and restart the branch from `origin/main` first — the prior push merged and the auto-merge workflow adds a `[skip ci]` SHA-tracker commit after every merge.

### 7.15 · Paste-in brief for Phase 4 row 5 (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-13 at v05.57r, at the close of row 4

Phase 4 is **four of twenty-six**. Rows 1–4 have settled the shape of a run, so this brief only records what the last one added.

**The checker signature is predictable, and row 5 breaks the recent pattern.** Rows 3 and 4 both inserted mid-track and so reported **P1 + P5**. Row 5's lesson joins `electrical-foundations`, which holds only `four-machines` today, so its §4 position 3 *is* an append among the built lessons — expect **P1 alone, no P5 and no P3**. A P5 on row 5 means the insertion went somewhere it should not have; anything else means stop and read why.

**One standing job this row should take, because it is now blocking a checker.** A `proscons` card's `t` and `meta` reach the DOM through `clEl()`'s `textContent`, not `clFmt` — and so do a `timeline`'s `lanes` values, a `bars` item's `label` and `sub`, a section's `title` and `read`, and the lesson's own `title`, `short`, `tiles[]` and `glossary[]`. A `{{term}}`, `**bold**` or `*italic*` in any of them prints its literal punctuation to the reader. Row 4 found this in a screenshot of its own lesson, removed its nine occurrences before commit, and found **nine already shipped**: `bridge-power` (`ways-to-eat-the-same-gas` cards 0, 2, 3 — `meta`), `the-control-stack` (`following-or-forming` cards 0 and 1 — `t`, and card 1 — `meta`), `where-bess-plugs-in` (`three-sockets` cards 0 and 1 — `t`) and `the-800-vdc-shift` (`glossary[6].d`, a `*square*`). **Fix those nine and then add the check** — the mirror of the v05.56r markup check, over the plain-field set above — in that order, because the check errors on them and a Classroom run's content-checker error count must stay byte-identical to a pristine `HEAD`. The wording change owes **no** `revisions[].changed[]` entry (G4: meaning does not move, and these are not `sections[]` fields a completed tick can be reconciled against), which is exactly why row 4 did not bundle it: editing another row's literal on a lesson-authoring commit adds a P8 finding to a run whose signature is the thing the next run reads. Expect **P1 + P8** on the commit that does it, and say so in its own note.

**What the last four runs established, so this one does not rediscover it.** The render harness answers the ops from the **real serving functions** — the PROJECT region run in Node, `clTrackIndexFor_` / `clLessonIndexFor_` / `clTrackFor_` / `clStudyNext_` replayed through a `window._gasPost` stub — which is what makes the render evidence of what the server would send rather than of a fixture. `clStudyNext_` is the proof a lesson is on the teaching walk: run it three ways — fresh, with every *other* lesson completed (it must then point at the new one), and with the new lesson completed too (it must move on) — and a mid-track insertion also shows whose pointer moved. The Actions log API truncates before the Deploy step on a run this size, so the authoritative deploy check is `curl` against `?action=api&op=deploy`. The EST clock, not UTC, governs the CHANGELOG rotation rule. And **the screenshots are not a formality** — the two defects found in this corpus so far (nested emphasis at row 3, plain-field markup at row 4) were both invisible to every checker and both visible in the first screenshot of the section that carried them.

Expect either a clean first-leg confirmation as usual, or a `POLL <n>` line — and if you see the latter, say so in your §7 note, because it is the evidence the poll earned its place. **Confirm independently either way**, since the Actions log API truncates before the Deploy step on a run this size: `curl "https://script.google.com/macros/s/$(jq -r .DEPLOYMENT_ID googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.config.json)/exec?action=api&op=deploy"` — `Already up to date (<version>)` means it landed — and `https://lightaisolutions.github.io/Sales/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt` for the Pages side. **A red gate still skips the Pages deploy entirely** (`deploy` needs `auto-merge.result == 'success'`), so if GAS is current and Pages is stale, the recovery is a **`workflow_dispatch` on `main`**: `auto-merge` is gated `github.ref != 'refs/heads/main'` so it skips, and `deploy` fires through its `auto-merge.result == 'skipped' && is-initialized` branch. Run #569 did exactly that and went green.
>

**Phase 4 is still 5 of 26 and row 6 is still the lowest unbuilt row.** S2 ran in between and touched a different artefact class entirely; nothing in Phase 4 moved. The corpus is **35 lessons, 8 tracks** — row 6 makes it 36.

One new data point in its favour: S2 session 1's landscape module rendered a **four-column, eighteen-row** table cleanly at a 1400px viewport, so the defect is about column count and first-column word length rather than row count.

**Two renderer traps that no checker catches, both found by reading screenshots.** First, the plain-field markup check added at v05.61r is live and is an **error**, not a warning: `{{term}}`, `**bold**` and `*italic*` in any field the renderer prints as text — a `proscons` card's `t` and `meta`, a section's `title` and `read`, `tiles[]`, `glossary[]`, a `revisions[].note`, a lesson's own `title`/`short`. **`epc-versus-gc` is a `proscons` section**, so this row writes exactly the fields that shipped the original defect four times: keep `t` and `meta` plain and put emphasis in `adv`/`dis`, which *are* formatted. Second, and newly measured at v05.64r: **`clFmt` resolves `**bold**`, `*italic*` and `{{term}}` and nothing else — a backtick renders literally to the reader**, and no checker scans for one. S2 session 1 shipped 254 of them into a module literal and caught them only in the screenshot. If you want code-ish emphasis in a lesson, use `**bold**`.

**The content contract is under real pressure on this row, and the tell is a proper noun.** `who-buys-storage` is a **mechanism lesson**: a company is a worked example, never the answer. A card may ask *what an IPP buys on, and who signs* — never *which company is an IPP*, and never *how many gigawatt-hours anyone shipped*. That drilling belongs to the segment lessons and the roster deck, and this row is neither. The amended contract in `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` says the same thing in one line; §7.2 decision 1 is what moved that fence, and it moved it for segment lessons only.

**The harness, so this row does not rediscover it.** Run the PROJECT region of `Classroom.gs` in Node with GAS shims and replay the **real** serving functions — `clRoleOf_`, `clTrackIndexFor_`, `clLessonIndexFor_`, `clTrackFor_`, `clLesson_`, `clStampKinds_`, `clLessonGate_`, `clStudyNext_` — through a `window._gasPost` stub, so the render is evidence of what the server would send. Serve a scratch copy over `http://127.0.0.1` with `_e = ''` and `AUTO_REFRESH = false`; seed the session **after** page load (`sessionStorage`, keys `Classroom_gas_session_token` / `_gas_user_email` / `_gas_user_role` / `_gas_user_permissions` — single-tab enforcement clears storage on load); override `window._gasPost` **after** load too; and **hide** `#auth-wall` / `.splash` / `#gas-pill` / `#verify-overlay` with CSS rather than `remove()`ing them, because removing makes unrelated page code dereference a null element and raises a `pageerror` that has nothing to do with your lesson. Copy `profiler-data/profiler-concepts.json`, `sounds/` and `images/` into the scratch site or the console fills with 404s. **One Playwright gotcha measured at v05.64r:** `page.evaluate(fn, arg)` passes exactly **one** argument — an arrow function written `(a, b) => …` silently receives the whole array as `a` and `undefined` as `b`, and the page then renders nothing with zero errors. Destructure: `([a, b]) => …`. Prove the lesson is on the teaching walk with `clStudyNext_` three ways — fresh, with every *other* lesson completed, and with this one completed — and, because this row inserts, show whose pointer moved.

### 7.19 · Paste-in brief for S2 session 2 — `cells-and-chemistry` (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-14 at v05.67r, at the close of Phase 4 row 6

**Why this and not Phase 4 row 7.** Phase 4 is **6 of 26** and S2 is **1 of ~19**, so the interleave stands at 6:1 and §7.4 item 6 puts the long lanes in alternation. Row 7 (`inside-the-rack`) is the lowest unbuilt mechanism row and is not going anywhere; the landscape lane has run once. This session is S2's second, and its segment is fixed by §7.3's sales-value order, which reads *integrators, cells, developers and IPPs, …*.

**A measurement correction to carry, because two places in this file get it wrong.** §7.3 describes cells as "the two deepest at 32 and 22 members" and §7.18's closing line calls it "the second-deepest segment". **Re-measured 2026-09-14 from `profiler-segments.json`: `cells-and-chemistry` holds 22 members and is the FIFTH-deepest segment**, behind `storage-developers-and-ipps` (34), `storage-integrators-and-containers` (32), `aidc-developers-and-landlords` (30) and `in-hall-power` (27). It is **second in the S2 order**, which is a sales-value ranking, not a depth ranking — the two were the same number at S0 and S3 has since deepened three other segments past it. The roster is **4 incumbent · 11 challenger · 7 adjacent**: incumbents `byd` · `catl` · `hithium` · `lg-energy-solution`; challengers `calb` · `cornex` · `envision-energy` · `eve-energy` · `form-energy` · `gotion` · `great-power` · `narada` · `rept` · `samsung-sdi` · `sunwoda`; adjacent `albemarle` · `canadian-solar` · `mitra-chem` · `novonix` · `panasonic` · `tesla` · `trina-storage`. **Re-measure it yourself on the day** — these are this session's numbers, not the pin, and S3's fills moved them once already.

**It is the only segment whose challengers outnumber its incumbents nearly three to one**, and that shape is the module's subject rather than a statistic about it: the `each-players-bet` table will run **fifteen rows** of incumbent-plus-challenger, and the dominance section has to explain how four incumbents hold a segment that eleven challengers are contesting. The segment's own `buyingCriteria` in the registry names six, and the fourth — *prohibited-foreign-entity status and the factory's location, status following ownership rather than site* — is the one that does the most work in this segment and the one a landscape module can get wrong by restating a headline.

**What S2 session 1 established that this session should not rediscover.** The module is authored **below the `// CONTENT END` fence** in `guidanceDocs_()`, not inside it — it is a guidance module, not a lesson, and the fence is the lesson pipeline's write set. It is corpus synthesis over member dossiers at the `profileVersion` its claims ledger pins, with the analysis file in `repository-information/industry-guidance/` as the source of truth; no ingested document and no new web research. **`clFmt` resolves only `**bold**`, `*italic*` and `{{term}}` — a backtick renders literally to the reader and no checker scans for one.** S2 session 1 shipped 254 of them into a ledger's Source column and caught them only in the screenshot; the backticked form belongs in the analysis markdown and the module gets plain text. **`reviewBy` is a written judgment, not a sort** — session 1 wrote its reasoning out in §10 of its analysis file because the generator's `review_by()` only accepts a full `YYYY-MM-DD` and cannot see a partial date, so a hand-authored module may resolve one and must say why.

**The regeneration this session owes, and the one it does not.** §10.6 requires the session to run `scripts/build-classroom-segments.py --segment cells-and-chemistry` **after** the module exists, so the segment lesson's `read-next` stops saying "No landscape module yet". That is one `--segment` invocation, well inside P10's caps. **It does not clear the other due segment, and should not try.** `--check` today reports **1 due: `storage-developers-and-ipps`, one string in `read-next`, no pin moved** — row 6's expected residue, because registering `who-buys-storage` turned a `PLANNED_TITLES` entry rendered as *"Eight Buyer Classes" (planned)* into the live title. That segment's own S2 session clears it. **So expect `--check` to read 1 due before you start, 2 due after the module lands, and 1 due after your one regeneration** — and say so in the record, because a session that reads 1 due as damage will regenerate a lesson that is not its to write.

**The baselines to capture BEFORE any edit, all measured at the close of v05.67r.** `check-classroom-content.py` is **0 errors / 0 warnings at 36 lessons, 8 tracks, 142 gate cases** — there is nothing inherited in that output, so any error is unambiguously yours. The checker also asserts the **registered guidance-module count is 10**; your module makes it **11**, so that assertion must be updated in the same commit — this is the first S2 session for which it is not already correct, since session 1 moved it from 9 to 10. `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` reports no structural findings. `check-classroom-pipeline.py --selftest` is 13 fixtures / 0 failures. **Expect P1 and P10 on `--base origin/main` and no P3**: the generator touches no `GATE_SYMBOLS` member, so `gateDigest` stays untouched, and a P3 would mean something else moved.

**THE DEPLOY — an instruction this brief had to correct on its own first use, at a measured cost of one deployment version.** The standing advice has been "confirm independently **after** the Deploy step has finished, or you race it and burn a deployment version". **That condition is necessary and not sufficient, and following it exactly still burned one at v05.67r:** the Deploy Classroom step reported `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.33g (deployment 43) | 43/200`, and the independent probe issued several minutes *after* that step completed answered `Updated to v01.33g (deployment 44) | 44/200` — a second deploy, not a confirmation. **The reason is in `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()` and is worth knowing exactly:** it fetches `Classroom.gs` from GitHub, reads the pulled `VERSION`, and compares it against the `VERSION` constant **of the code currently executing**; equal returns `Already up to date (…)` and does nothing, unequal *deploys*. Between a deployment being repointed and `/exec` actually executing the new bundle there is a propagation lag, so a probe inside that window runs under the **old** `VERSION`, sees a mismatch against GitHub's new one, and deploys again. **The correct order, which costs nothing:** check Pages first — `https://lightaisolutions.github.io/Sales/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt` is a static file and can be read as often as you like — and read the workflow's own `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET)` line out of the job log, which is the authoritative statement that the deploy landed. **Probe `?op=deploy` only if one of those two disagrees**, accept that it may deploy once, and re-probe after: a second probe returning `Already up to date (v01.33g)` is how v05.67r established that the state had settled and that the endpoint is idempotent once `/exec` is running the new code. Use `curl -sL` when you do probe — the endpoint redirects and without `-L` the body is empty, which reads as a failure.

**Versions and counters to bump from.** Classroom GAS **v01.33g** (both `Classroom.gs` `VERSION` and `live-site-pages/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt`); page **v01.14w**, and no page bump is expected. Repo **v05.69r**. Deployment counter **44/200** — 43 from the workflow's own deploy and 44 from the confirmation probe described above. v05.64r and v05.67r both confirmed on the **first GET leg with no `POLL <n>` line**, so the v05.59r poll is armed and **has still never been needed**; report it if you see one.

**CHANGELOGS — read the counters and do the non-exempt arithmetic.** Use `TZ=America/New_York date '+%Y-%m-%d'`. The rule counts **non-exempt** sections (total minus today's), not the raw counter; §7.17 read the raw number twice and was wrong twice, and §7.18 got it right by spelling out both branches. **(a) The repo CHANGELOG stands at 109 raw / 98 non-exempt, eleven sections dated 2026-09-14** — the count this brief's own commit leaves behind, not the count when the brief was first written. A push landing 2026-09-14 does **not** rotate. A push landing **2026-09-15 or later ROTATES** — the 2026-09-14 group stops being exempt, the count clears 100, and the oldest whole group to move is **eighteen sections dated 2026-09-05 (`v04.61r`–`v04.78r`)**, leaving 91. One group is enough. **Re-count rather than trusting this line**: `grep -c '^## \[v' repository-information/CHANGELOG.md` minus the same grep narrowed to today's EST date is the whole rule, and three briefs in a row have now had to correct a written-down counter. **(b) `Classroomgs.changelog.md` is at 33/50, 29 non-exempt** — no rotation either way. **(c) `Scrapergs.changelog.md` reads 50/50 with 49 non-exempt**, so the next session to touch `Scraper.gs` on a later EST day fires rotation there (oldest group: twenty-six sections dated 2026-08-28); `Profilerhtml.changelog.md` sits at 49/50. **If this session adds a Scraper seed as session 1 did, that trap is yours.** SHA enrichment is mandatory on every moved header, and `git fetch --unshallow origin main` is the precondition, not the remedy — a remote session's clone is shallow and unshallowing does not persist between containers.

**Two open items recorded and not taken, neither of them this session's to fix.** The `.cl-tbl` `min-width` question is still open but has lost most of its force: row 6's **five-column, eleven-row** table rendered cleanly at 1400px, as did session 1's four-column, eighteen-row landscape table, so the defect looks to be about a first column holding one long word rather than about column or row count. And the generated segment lesson's `read-next` still **names** the landscape module without linking to it — the generator emits the id as italic text and the `cl*` renderer has no anchor syntax. §10.6 calls it a deep link; today it is a stated id. That is a generator-template question, and §10.4 forbids hand-editing a generated literal.

### 7.23 · Paste-in brief for S2 session 4 — `utilities` (Opus 5 xhigh) — written 2026-09-14 at v05.76r, at the close of Phase 4 row 8

**Why this and not another Phase 4 row.** Phase 4 is now **8 of 26** and S2 is **3 of ~19**, so the interleave stands at 8:3 and §7.4 item 6 alternates the two long lanes. It is S2's turn, and `utilities` is the next segment by the §7.3 sales-value order. Phase 4's row 9 (`the-campus-as-a-power-project`) is not going anywhere.

**The segment, measured on 2026-09-14 rather than forecast: 14 members, 6 · 2 · 6.** Six incumbents (`dominion-energy`, `southern-company`, `entergy`, `oncor`, `aep`, `xcel-energy`), two challengers (`nrg-energy`, `vistra` — both competitive **retail** suppliers rather than wires companies) and six adjacents (`engie-north-america`, `tesla`, `nextera-energy-resources`, `invenergy`, `grid-united`, `pattern-energy`). **Re-measure on the day** — three of the last four S2 sessions found the registry had moved under a written-down figure, and this one was exact, which is the less common outcome. The `notes` field records that the reserved adjacent role was filled at V3 (v05.40r) by the two transmission developers, so the adjacency story here is *ownership slices and build-own-transfer*, not generation.

**The one structural thing that makes this segment different from the first three landscapes: a guidance module already covers part of it.** `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` is registered and is the **playbook** — how an AI load is procured through a utility. `landscape-utilities-2026-09` is the **market-structure judgment layer** over the same franchises: who dominates and on what basis, who threatens, each player's bet. Read the existing module first and write the landscape so the two are explicitly non-overlapping, the way `vertiv` and `schneider-electric`'s UPS spines were made to state their split in each other's opening callout. A landscape that re-tells the procurement playbook is the predictable failure here, exactly as re-teaching the UPS was row 8's.

**The second structural thing: the challengers are not a smaller version of the incumbents.** The two challengers are competitive retail suppliers and four of the six adjacents sell *to* or *through* the incumbents rather than against them. The `storage-developers-and-ipps` finding — that depth is not concentration, and that a threat section padded to the previous module's proportions would have missed it — applies here with a different shape: the threat to a regulated wires franchise is not another wires franchise, it is disintermediation of the load. Let the section proportions follow the segment rather than the last module.

**The Scraper-seed question must be asked, and the answer is not obviously yes.** S2 session 1 and session 3 each added one seed; session 2 did not, because an existing seed already scored its layer. Check every candidate term against **all** the seeds already in `SCRAPER_SEGMENT_SEEDS` before proposing one — session 3's first draft proposed two terms that `topic-storage-offtake` and `topic-capacity-markets` already carried. The utility layer is well covered by existing rate-case and procurement seeds; a new seed needs a scoring gap it can name, and a separate ranking table or filing class behind it. **A `tv` marker is needed only when an existing seed's terms are edited, never for a new key.**

**THE DUE-COUNT PREDICTION, and row 8 corrected the mechanism the last three briefs described.** `--check` reads **5 due** as you start — `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` and `compute-and-the-rack` (row 7's residue) plus `in-hall-power`, `aidc-developers-and-landlords` and `neoclouds` (row 8's). **None of them is yours and none of them is `utilities`.** Registering `landscape-utilities-2026-09` will make `segment-utilities` go due on `read-next` — the generator's `sec_read_next` renders "**No landscape module yet.**" until a `landscape-<segment-id>-YYYY-MM` id exists in `guidanceDocs_()`, then names it — so expect **5 → 6 due**, regenerate `--segment utilities` only, and **end back at 5**. Leave the other five alone: each belongs to its own S2 session under §10.9.

**And the correction row 8 made, which every future Phase 4 and S2 brief should inherit.** §7.22 described two independent channels by which registering a lesson makes segments due — `READ_NEXT` membership and the buying-criteria lexicon — and predicted 5 to 7. The count came in at 5, and the reason is that **the two channels are not independent: the lexicon is subordinate to `READ_NEXT`.** `sec_what_is_bought` guards its match with `if lid in eligible`, where `eligible` is `set(READ_NEXT.get(seg["id"], []))`, so a lesson can only alter `what-is-bought-and-on-what` in a segment whose `READ_NEXT` already names it. `grid-equipment` and `hyperscalers-and-ai-labs` both carry a criterion containing the word "availability" and neither could ever have gone due, because `redundancy-by-the-numbers` is not in their `READ_NEXT`. **The bound is |READ_NEXT membership| + prior residue**, and the lexicon only decides whether an eligible segment differs in one section or two.

**READ THIS BEFORE YOU TAKE A SINGLE PIN — the clone finding of v05.80r, and it is new.** A fresh container clones **shallow**, and a shallow clone's boundary commit looks like it introduced every file in the repo. So `git log -1 --format=%cs -- <path>` — which §8 item 2 and G2 make the pin source for `project:` and `concepts:` refs — **stops at the boundary and returns the boundary's date instead of the file's**. Row 9 met this cold: `build-classroom-segments.py --check` read **19 due** against a measured 5, every extra one reporting `inputs moved: concepts:profiler-concepts 2026-09-13→2026-09-14` with `sections differing: none`. `git fetch --unshallow origin main` restored the true answer (commit `5eab070`, **2026-09-13**) and `--check` fell straight back to 5. **The due count is only the alarm; the real cost is the pin.** Authoring off the shallow clone would have written `concepts:profiler-concepts@2026-09-14` into every stamp when the true pin is 2026-09-13 — invisible to every checker, and a false baseline for the next C2 refresh. **Unshallow first, before any measurement and before any pin.** Earlier briefs named unshallowing only as the precondition for CHANGELOG SHA enrichment; it is equally the precondition for the pin reads. If `--check` reports a number wildly above the one below, suspect the clone before you suspect the corpus.
